# 2026-11-MonthlyUpdate-CCF_A-Federated Learning All Paper Info

Star marker ⭐ indicates a privacy-preserving or robustness/security defense federated learning scheme. Pure attack, vulnerability, comment, or critique papers are not starred unless they also propose a defense scheme.

In this table, only focus on latest papers published in TIFS, TDSC, TMC, INFOCOM, WWW, TSC, USENIX Security.

| 1/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TIFS | A | `Label-Aware Distribution-Based Dynamic Weighting Aggregation for Hierarchical Backdoor Defense in Federated Learning` - Federated learning (FL) is vulnerable to backdoor attacks, where malicious clients inject poisoned updates into the global model through local training. Existing defenses mainly rely on anomaly detection, yet their effectiveness is often undermined by non-IID data distributions and the subtle similarity between benign and malicious updates. To address these challenges, we propose Label-Aware Distribution-based Dynamic Weighting (LADW), a novel black-box aggregation strategy that operates without any trusted client information. Leveraging a small auxiliary dataset, even one consisting of synthetic data generated by a large text-to-image model, LADW infers each client’s implicit label distribution from update similarity and dynamically adjusts aggregation weights to suppress label-concentrated, potentially malicious updates. Furthermore, LADW can be seamlessly integrated with existing detection-based defenses, forming an adaptive and hierarchical framework that combines detection, norm clipping, and dynamic aggregation, while also enabling inference of the adversary’s target label. Extensive experiments on multiple benchmarks demonstrate that LADW substantially enhances the effectiveness of existing defenses by reducing attack success rates while improving the global model’s main-task accuracy. Notably, under highly non-IID conditions, LADW achieves state-of-the-art performance, highlighting its robustness and adaptability across diverse federated learning settings. The source code is available at https://github.com/zalmanchen/LADW | Xi Chen; Chunyi Zhou; Rui Zeng; Xiaogang Xu; Jiafei Wu; Zhe Liu; Jianwei Yin; Jinbao Li; Shouling Ji | federated learning, backdoor defense, label distribution inference, dynamic weighting aggregation, non-IID | label-aware aggregation for backdoor defense | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIFS.2026.3744651) |
| 2/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TIFS | A | `Secure and Efficient Model Aggregation for Resilient Federated Learning` - Federated learning (FL) has received considerable attention for its distributed collaborative training properties, which can construct the global model without sharing private datasets of clients scenarios. In order to overcome data silos, clients individually train models on local data and share the model parameters with the server for global aggregation. In this paper, we propose a secure and efficient aggregation FL framework, namely FedSEA, which provides lightweight protection for clients’ local models, while allowing accurate aggregation of the protected coefficients. Specifically, the server applies truncated SVD to the initial global model and distributes the quantized retained bases to clients for local caching. Each client reuses these bases to project its residual-compensated local model and uploads only quantized pseudo-singular values rather than computing a local SVD or transmitting low-rank factors. The local residual preserves discarded information without increasing the transmitted payload. Moreover, FedSEA employs a pairwise and server-client symmetric masking noise, which is implemented through Diffie–Hellman-based shared key exchange and pseudo-random generator. The masks are removed exactly during coefficient aggregation, after which the server reconstructs the aggregated model. When selected clients drop out, FedSEA removes their residual masks and aggregates the remaining active clients, thereby preserving system resilience. Extensive theoretical analysis and experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of FedSEA in improving communication efficiency while preserving model utility and security. | Mengqian Li; Youliang Tian; Junpeng Zhang; Ruixin Song; Ze Yang; Jinbo Xiong; Jianfeng Ma | federated learning, secure aggregation, truncated SVD, pairwise masking, communication efficiency, dropout resilience | secure and communication-efficient model aggregation | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIFS.2026.3744614) |
| 3/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TIFS | A | `TAFL: Trust-Aware Federated Learning for Anomalous Client Detection, Isolation, and Recovery` - Federated Learning (FL) systems remain vulnerable to anomalous clients, including unreliable participants and malicious adversaries. Such clients can manipulate updates, poison data, exhibit non-IID drift, or participate intermittently, degrading model integrity, convergence, and trust. To address this, we propose Trust-Aware Federated Learning (TAFL), a unified framework for anomalous-client detection, probationary isolation, and controlled reintegration. TAFL combines Subjective Logic (SL) and Multi-Agent Systems (MAS) to provide uncertainty-aware trust estimation and peer-informed stabilization under dynamic participation. Targeting adaptive adversaries under bounded compromise capabilities, TAFL applies multi-signal consistency screening, including behavioral consistency and attribution drift, to detect subtle deviations beyond obvious outliers. Low-trust clients enter probationary isolation and are reintegrated only after sustained behavioral recovery. To promote fairness and deter malicious participation, TAFL enforces trust- and quality-aligned incentives with auditable smart-contract enforcement and provides theoretical analysis supporting convergence, trust recovery, and bounded adversarial influence. Experiments on MNIST and CIFAR10 show 2–4% higher anomalous-client detection and 20–25% lower convergence latency under 10–30% anomalous-client ratios. FEMNIST results further confirm robustness under stronger user-level heterogeneity, while stress tests on CIFAR10 show 94.6–97.1% mean detection and reduce semantic-backdoor attack success rate (ASR) to 2.9%. | Md Mamunur Rashid; Md Palash Uddin; Yong Xiang; Keshav Sood; Yushu Zhang; Longxiang Gao | federated learning, trust evaluation, subjective logic, multi-agent systems, anomalous client detection, smart contract | trust-aware anomalous client detection and recovery | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIFS.2026.3744607) |
| 4/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TIFS | A | `FGDPsyn: Federated Graph Synthesis With Differential Privacy` - This paper studies differentially private (DP) graph synthesis in federated settings, where each client holds a local subgraph. While existing DP graph synthesis methods assume a centralized data model, their direct deployment in federated environments suffers from severely degraded utility. To bridge this gap, we introduce FGDPsyn, a novel framework for federated DP-compliant graph synthesis. FGDPsyn treats the nodes within a client as an atomic unit and aggregates global structural statistics in a client-wise fashion. A key challenge in this setting is the linear growth of accumulated DP noise with the number of participating clients. To mitigate its impact on synthesis quality, FGDPsyn incorporates an adaptive threshold-based clipping technique that removes unreliable inter-subgraph edge statistics, thereby enhancing robustness to noise. We further propose a fine-grained link construction mechanism that progressively incorporates selected edges into inter-subgraph statistical estimation until a confidence threshold is met, naturally introducing heterogeneity in edge participation across the federation. To leverage this heterogeneity, we adopt a heterogeneous-aware information extraction mechanism that fully exploits the varied edge participation characteristics, thereby achieving near-complete utilization of the privacy budget. Experiments demonstrate that FGDPsyn generates synthetic graphs with high utility across multiple metrics. | Ning Wang; Yujuan Wei; Yuan Ji; Shen Su; Zhigang Wang; Yu Gu; Jia Xu; Zhihong Tian | federated graph synthesis, differential privacy, subgraph federation, adaptive clipping, privacy budget | differentially private federated graph synthesis | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIFS.2026.3746765) |
| 5/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TIFS | A | `Federated Quantum Learning With Adaptive Differential Privacy via Quantum Noise` - This paper studies a novel quantum federated learning (QFL) framework that leverages inherent quantum noise to enforce differential privacy (DP) and protect model updates from information leakage in current near-term quantum devices. Specifically, we achieve targeted DP guarantees during model training by i) varying the number of measurement shots, and ii) leveraging unavoidable depolarizing channel noise. To adapt privacy levels based on varying training conditions, we further introduce an adaptive DP strategy using a multi-armed bandit (MAB) agent at each quantum device, enabling dynamic control over the trade-off between privacy and model performance. Moreover, we present a rigorous convergence analysis of our proposed QFL-DP model. Our theoretical analysis and numerical evaluation reveal the following key properties: i) the global QFL-DP model converges to a bounded error floor, which indicates that, despite leveraging inherent quantum noise for DP, the model’s performance remains stable, making our approach suitable for real-world QFL applications; ii) a clear trade-off exists between privacy guarantees and model accuracy, and the adaptive MAB strategy further enhances this trade-off by enabling adaptive privacy budgeting with efficient noise allocation based on training dynamics and client needs; iii) our theoretical results are consistent with simulations, thereby demonstrating the superiority of the proposed framework over baseline approaches in terms of training accuracy, adaptive privacy preservation, and robustness against adversarial examples. Overall, the results highlight a tunable trade-off between privacy and robustness, offering an efficient and secure QFL solution for current near-term quantum devices. | Atit Pokharel; Shaba Shaon; Thomas Morris; Dinh C. Nguyen | quantum federated learning, differential privacy, quantum noise, multi-armed bandit, convergence analysis | adaptive differential privacy for quantum federated learning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIFS.2026.3750716) |
| 6/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TIFS | A | `GameRLFed: Robust Federated Learning Against Evolving Backdoor Attacks via Game Theory and Deep Reinforcement Learning` - Federated learning (FL) enables collaborative model training without directly sharing data, thereby offering effective privacy guarantees. However, the decentralized architecture of FL makes it vulnerable to backdoor attacks, where adversaries can compromise a subset of clients to inject malicious updates, causing the global model to misbehave on specific trigger inputs. The existing defenses typically rely on static rules or auxiliary validation data, which often lack adaptability against evolving attack strategies or introduce privacy risks and computational overhead. To address these challenges, we propose GameRLFed, a robust defense framework that formulates the FL security problem as a repeated game with incomplete information. Without requiring any auxiliary data, the server infers the malicious tendencies of each client based on current updates and historical observations and employs reinforcement learning to dynamically adjust the aggregation weights for mitigating backdoor effects. Moreover, attackers adaptively evolve their strategies in response to the behavior of the server to maximize the effectiveness of their attacks while minimizing the risk of detection, resulting in complex game interactions. | 7/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TIFS | A | `DP-HFGL: Heterogeneous Federated Graph Learning With Differential Privacy` - Federated Graph Learning (FGL) has garnered increasing interest thanks to its ability to harness the representational power of graphs, while its development is still impeded by privacy issues stemming from the direct upload of a participant’s local model. The primary challenges in this work stem from the intra-client feature and structure heterogeneity among graphs, inter-client statistical heterogeneity in local distribution shifts and DP-required noise injection. | 9/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `LDPAKE: A Unified PAKE–LDP–ZKP Framework for Verifiable Privacy in Federated Learning` - Federated learning enables collaborative model training without transferring raw data, but practical deployments still lack a unified mechanism that simultaneously provides deployable mutual authentication, context-bound uploads, and verifiable client-side privacy protection. The resulting design preserves three properties in a unified manner: lightweight mutual authentication without PKI or trusted hardware, explicit binding of each upload to its session and round context, and verifiable client-side perturbation without revealing the plaintext update or the realized noise. | 17/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `A Privacy-Preserving Federated Learning-Driven Secure Replication and Multiobjective Resource Optimization Framework for Edge Computing` - In edge computing, data Replication Coding (RC) represents the overarching redundancy management paradigm for guaranteeing data availability and reliability, while Erasure Coding (EC) constitutes a more storage-efficient redundancy mechanism for implementing this paradigm. | 24/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `Certified Unlearning in Decentralized Federated Learning` - Driven by the right to be forgotten (RTBF), machine unlearning has become an essential requirement for privacy-preserving machine learning. | 43/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `Federated Unlearning via Representation Misdirection with Adaptive Anchor Generation` - Data-protection regulations such as the GDPR grant every participant in a federated system a right to be forgotten. Federated unlearning has therefore emerged as a research frontier, aiming to remove a specific party's contribution from the learned model while preserving the utility of the remaining parties. However, most unlearning techniques focus on Horizontal Federated Learning (HFL), where data are partitioned by samples. In contrast, Vertical Federated Learning (VFL) allows organizations that possess complementary feature spaces to train a joint model without sharing raw data. The resulting feature-partitioned architecture renders HFL-oriented unlearning methods ineffective. In this paper, we propose ReMisVFU, a plug-and-play representation misdirection framework that enables fast, client-level unlearning in splitVFL systems. When a deletion request arrives, the forgetting party collapses its encoder output to a generated anchor on the unit sphere, severing the statistical link between its features and the global model. To maintain utility for the remaining parties, the server jointly optimizes a retention loss and a forgetting loss, aligning their gradients via orthogonal projection to eliminate destructive interference. | 44/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `FLFaaSPHE: A Lightweight Privacy-Preserving Federated Learning Framework for Serverless Computing Environments` - The Function as a Service (FaaS) paradigm, through abstracting cloud resource management, is regarded as the next-generation cloud computing system. | 45/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `FedCamo: Secure and Dependable Federated Learning With Model Camouflage` - AI driven applications are entering safety critical domains and increasingly rely on federated learning (FL) to coordinate model training across many devices. | 46/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `SecureDFL: A Secure Distributed Federated Learning Framework Against Poisoning Attacks` - Distributed Federated Learning (DFL) extends federated learning from a single-server topology to broader graph-based coordination settings that may include multiple interacting aggregation nodes and, in some deployments, externally distributed training resources. | 48/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `Poisoning-Assisted Membership Inference in Federated Learning` - Federated learning (FL) is a distributed machine learning approach that allows multiple clients to collaboratively train a global model without sharing their local raw data. | 50/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `Private Metadata-Driven Retrieval-Augmented Reconstruction for Missing Modalities in Multimodal Federated Learning` - Multimodal federated learning (MFL) enables clients with distributed multimodal data to collaboratively train models while preserving privacy, and has been widely used in multi-domain IoT and blockchain-enabled applications where clients collect heterogeneous data such as images, text, and sensor signals. | 51/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `Robust and Explainable Federated Learning via Semantic Concept Bottlenecks: A Security-Enhanced Approach` - Federated Learning (FL) enables collaborative model training across decentralized data sources while preserving user privacy. | 54/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `When Blockchain and Federated Learning Hit it Off: Forging a Novel Proof of Useful Work` - Traditional Proof-of-Work (PoW) consensus secures blockchains by incurring significant energy and resource wastage on solving valueless puzzles. However, existing PoUW approaches face a critical trade-off between the work’s practical utility and its verifiability, as efficiently validating complex computations without full re-execution remains a major open problem. This paper proposes Proof-of-Useful-Federated-Work (PoUFW), a novel consensus protocol that resolves this dilemma by recasting the “work” as Federated Learning (FL) model aggregation. Specifically, PoUFW mandates that miners not only perform the aggregation, but also generate a cryptographic proof of its correctness. To achieve this, we propose a novel proof mechanism that builds upon the Kate-Zaverucha-Goldberg (KZG) polynomial commitment scheme to generate a concise, non-interactive proof. This mechanism enables validators to rapidly verify the aggregation’s integrity without re-executing the computation or accessing the underlying model. Consequently, PoUFW transforms the computational overhead of consensus into valuable, verifiable machine learning outcomes, while simultaneously establishing a decentralized, incentivized framework for FL. Rigorous theoretical analysis substantiates the protocol’s security and correctness. Extensive simulations further demonstrate that PoUFW achieves high verification efficiency alongside robust security, confirming its practical feasibility and scalability. | 58/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `Unlearning or Not: A Strategic Data Forgetting Scheme for Federated Unlearning With Bounded Rationality` - Federated unlearning (FUL) is a promising approach that enables users to remove the influence of their data from federated learning (FL) models, thereby safeguarding their right to be forgotten and protecting data privacy. To tackle the aforementioned challenges, we propose a strategic data forgetting scheme with bounded rationality for FUL. Specifically, we first present a practical FUL framework where a boundedly rational FL server permits clients to perform partial data revocation. Then, to incentivize clients to retain more data during the unlearning process, we develop a truthful incentive mechanism based on a prospect theory (PT)-driven data forgetting game, which analyzes the FL server’s optimal incentive strategy and the clients’ corresponding data revocation decisions. By employing PT, the boundedly rational decision-making process of the FL server is effectively modeled, capturing its behavioral responses under conditions of uncertainty and risk. Furthermore, a penalty mechanism is proposed to mitigate clients’ selfish behavior, complemented by a zero-payment mechanism designed to ensure equitable distribution of rewards among clients. | 59/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `Federated Graph Analytics With Differential Privacy` - Collaborative graph analysis across multiple institutions is becoming increasingly popular. | 60/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `FedAggVerify: Publicly Verifiable and Privacy-Preserving Aggregation With Ownership Protection for Federated Learning` - We present ${\sf FedAggVerify}$, a novel single-server federated learning (FL) aggregation protocol and the first to provide publicly verifiable, non-interactive integrity proofs for each training round while preserving client-update privacy and participation anonymity, even when the aggregator is malicious and colludes with compromised clients. Prior publicly verifiable aggregation schemes either lose soundness under server–client collusion or rely on helper-mediated co-signing and recovery procedures that place an adversarial server on the critical path and create availability and communication bottlenecks. ${\sf FedAggVerify}$ makes inconsistencies between the accepted client contributions and the released aggregate externally verifiable, supports off-path forensic auditing, and binds released models to the owner through an Error Correction Code-protected signature watermark for intellectual-property protection. Experiments using real cryptographic primitives and large model dimensions demonstrate practical efficiency and substantially lower communication, verification, and dropout-recovery overhead than prior publicly verifiable aggregation baselines while maintaining verifiable watermark recovery. | Wafaa Kanakri; Brian King | federated learning, publicly verifiable aggregation, secure aggregation, signature watermark, ownership protection, collusion resistance | publicly verifiable private aggregation with ownership protection | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TDSC.2026.3717223) |
| 61/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `GUARD: Mitigating Backdoors in Federated Learning Via Influence-Guided Unlearning` - Federated learning (FL) is increasingly vulnerable to backdoor attacks, where malicious participants inject poisoned updates to implant hidden behaviors within the global model. Existing defenses often rely on ad-hoc aggregation rules, heuristic parameter modifications, or external trusted datasets, severely limiting their robustness, interpretability, and transferability across diverse architectures and attack modalities. To address these fundamental limitations, we propose a Guided Unlearning for Adversarial Robustness and Defense (i.e., GUARD). It is a unified, data-centric framework, which leverages the principles of machine unlearning to systematically secure FL systems. Specifically, GUARD establishes a rigorous theoretical link between data influence estimation and backdoor mitigation. It utilizes fast influence-function approximation to efficiently isolate poisoned training contributions without requiring auxiliary clean data. Building on this precise detection, the framework executes a sequential, closed-loop defense pipeline: an influence-guided unlearning stage that explicitly neutralizes malicious parameter biases through reverse optimization, followed by a stability-preserving lightweight fine-tuning stage on internally verified clean data to seamlessly restore standard predictive performance. Comprehensive experiments across diverse datasets, advanced backdoor attacks, and model architectures demonstrate that GUARD achieves superior threat eradication and clean accuracy preservation compared to state-of-the-art baselines. | 62/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `Differentially Private Decoupled Clustered Federated Learning Under Mixed Heterogeneity` - Federated Learning (FL) facilitates collaborative model training without sharing local data, yet it grapples with issues of gradient leakage and data heterogeneity. | 76/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `FedForward: Efficient Federated Learning With Back Propagation-Free Training and Privacy Guarantee` - In Federated Learning (FL), stragglers refer to the slow devices with delayed updates that stall model aggregations at the server. | 78/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Long-Term Optimal Incentives for Differential-Privacy Federated Learning: A Multi-Stage Game Approach` - Differential-privacy federated learning (DP-FL) has emerged as a promising paradigm capable of mitigating the inherent threat of traditional FL architectures that are vulnerable to inferential attacks due to the frequent exchange and updating of model parameters. | 80/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `UOIM: Online Incentive Design for Fair Hierarchical Federated Learning and Unlearning in Edge Computing` - Hierarchical Federated Learning (HFL) has emerged as a promising evolution of Federated Learning (FL) where a shared model is learned collaboratively via hierarchical aggregation, and it requires federated unlearning to protect users’ right to be forgotten in the training process. However, most existing designs neglect the unfair issue in federated unlearning, i.e., it is intuitively unfair for remaining users to spend additional costs to achieve the unlearning of target data of leaving users. This issue becomes even more serious in the dynamical HFL scenarios, where multi-level aggregation involves more intermediate users that may leave at any time, and the consecutive leaving of users will accumulate unfair costs for subsequent remaining users. Herein, we aim to bridge this gap via an unlearning-aware online incentive design. First, we design an online learning-based repeated auction to model the dynamics of the real-time participant selection process, where the multi-armed bandit machine is used to dynamically select participants in each round and assign the response reward. Moreover, we introduce a membership inference-based unlearning operation into the auction model for revoking target data of leaving users. Rigorous theoretical analysis and extensive experimental results show the effectiveness of the proposed mechanism and provide effective insights and strategies for the resource trading in the HFL system to complete the federated training. | Ying Qian; Lianbo Ma; Guo Yu; Meng Zheng; Min Huang; Qing Li; Xingwei Wang | hierarchical federated learning, federated unlearning, incentive mechanism, online auction, multi-armed bandit | unlearning-aware incentive design for hierarchical federated learning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3712254) |
| 81/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `OFDM-Assisted Over-the-Air Computation With Privacy Protection in Federated Learning` - Federated Learning (FL) is an emerging distributed machine learning paradigm that protects data privacy by performing iterative local training and gradient aggregation across multiple devices and a central server. Over-the-air computation enables fast aggregation when multiple devices need to upload their local gradients to the central server simultaneously. Orthogonal Frequency Division Modulation (OFDM) further accelerates this process by allowing concurrent symbol transmission over multiple fading channels. To address the challenge of non-linear distortion at the OFDM transmitter’s amplifier while ensuring privacy for each device, we propose a novel aggregation method: OFDM-assisted over-the-air computation for differential privacy (DP) in FL (OA2DPFL). This method first normalizes local gradients and then adds uniformly distributed artificial noise before uploading them to the central server. Our analysis demonstrates that the proposed approach effectively controls peak transmit power and establishes a quantitative relationship between the variance of artificial noise and the level of DP protection. Furthermore, we rigorously prove that OA2DPFL achieves a zero stationary optimality gap under general $\displaystyle L$-smooth (non-convex) loss functions, and a zero global optimality gap under $\displaystyle L$-smooth and strongly convex loss functions, both with a convergence rate of $\displaystyle \mathcal {O}(1/T^{1/2-\rho })$, where $\displaystyle T$ is the iteration number and $\displaystyle \rho \in (0,1/2)$. Experimental results validate the superiority of OA2DPFL in terms of peak power, convergence performance, and DP protection compared to baseline methods. | Shiyuan Zuo; Rongfei Fan; Puning Zhao; Cheng Zhan; Han Hu; Zehui Xiong | federated learning, over-the-air computation, OFDM, differential privacy, convergence analysis | differentially private OFDM-assisted over-the-air federated learning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3715532) |
| 82/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Subspace-Guided Unlearning and Recovery: Poisoning Defense for Hierarchical Federated Learning in LEO Satellite Networks` - Low Earth Orbit (LEO) satellite networks are increasingly adopting Federated Learning (FL) for privacy-preserving collaborative model training. | 83/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `A Federated Learning Framework Based on Distributed Homomorphic Encryption for Cross-Trust Domain Collaboration in Vehicular Networks` - With the rapid development of artificial intelligence and new energy technologies, intelligent driving has become a core component of modern transportation infrastructure. | 84/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Adaptive Federated Unlearning in Heterogeneous Edge Networks via Contrastive Fine-Tuning` - Federated unlearning (FU) has become a critical capability of privacy-sensitive edge intelligence, which enables mobile and Internet of Things (IoT) devices to revoke previously contributing data from collaboratively trained models. To address this limitation, we propose an adaptive federated unlearning framework tailored for heterogeneous edge environments. Through a representation-level analysis, we characterize how data heterogeneity shapes forgetting behaviors and identify two key factors that hinder unlearning performance: directed representation drift and heterogeneity-induced hard samples. Guided by these insights, we develop contrastive federated unlearning (CFU), a two-stage framework that integrates local contrastive fine-tuning with global model calibration. CFU employs contrastive unlearning to induce targeted representation shifts, whereas lightweight fine-tuning mitigates the adverse effects of heterogeneity. The unlearning process is further safeguarded through localized forgetting and data-free knowledge distillation. Extensive experiments on both client and sample-level unlearning tasks demonstrate that CFU consistently outperforms state-of-the-art approximate unlearning methods across diverse real-world datasets, achieving a 2–4× efficiency improvement over retraining. | Xiao Jiang; Hong Zhong; Depeng Chen; Jie Cui | federated unlearning, data heterogeneity, contrastive learning, fine-tuning, edge networks | adaptive federated unlearning under heterogeneity | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3725778) |
| 85/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Upcycling Noise for Federated Unlearning` - In Federated Learning (FL), multiple clients collaboratively train a model without sharing raw data. Additionally, for the efficient and consistent implementation of the proposed FUI, we formulate a two-stage Stackelberg game to derive optimal unlearning strategies for both the server and the target client. | 86/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TSC | A | `PoFEL: Energy-Efficient Consensus for Blockchain-Based Hierarchical Federated Learning` - Facilitated by mobile edge computing, client-edge- cloud hierarchical federated learning (HFL) enables communication-efficient model training in a widespread area but also incurs additional security and privacy challenges from intermediate model aggregations and remains vulnerable to the single point of failure issue. | 87/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TSC | A | `DUAL: A Federated Unsupervised Anomaly Detection Framework for Collaborative Business Processes` - Detecting anomalies in business processes is imperative for achieving operational success, especially as multiple participants increasingly engage in collaborative efforts to complete processes, i.e., collaborative business processes, amidst rapid economic development. | 88/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TSC | A | `AdaFUN: Enhancing Federated Unlearning With Adaptive Local Step in Edge Computing` - Federated Unlearning (FU) has emerged as a pivotal paradigm for ensuring the “Right To Be Forgotten” in contemporary intelligent services of Edge Computing (EC). | 89/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TSC | A | `FedAtlas: A Privacy-Enhanced Federated Multi-Task Learning Framework Driven by Semantic Communication for Dual Heterogeneity` - Federated Multi-Task Learning (FMTL) faces fundamental challenges arising from dual heterogeneity, including intrinsic task heterogeneity and communication challenges induced by heterogeneous system environments. | 90/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TSC | A | `Privacy-Preserving and Verifiable Federated Learning Framework for Biometric Authentication at the Edge` - Federated learning (FL) has emerged as a pivotal privacy-preserving paradigm for training models across decentralized biometric datasets. | 91/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TSC | A | `Shielding Federated Learning Services From a Novel Stealthy Label-Flipping Attack Exposing Membership` - Label-flipping attacks pose a significant threat to the security and privacy of machine learning models, particularly in federated learning (FL). | 92/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TSC | A | `FairWeight: Private Fairness-Aware Aggregation Using Model Interpretation Based Weightage in Federated Learning` - Federated learning (FL) has been adopted as a promising solution for collaborative privacy-preserving machine learning. Thus, a global model, even with high accuracy, may be prone to discrimination and perform well only for certain sensitive attributes and classes. Global accuracy is not a perfect measure of the model’s effectiveness; it should be reported in terms of balanced accuracy and fairness. Existing solutions attempt to mitigate bias using local or global debiasing techniques. However, local debiasing results in poor performance with increasing heterogeneity, and global debiasing is either designed only for achieving fairness, ignoring balanced accuracy, or costly in terms of finding an optimal solution. We present a novel model interpretation based bias mitigation aggregation scheme in FL, called FairWeight. It calculates the aggregation weightage by considering both fairness and loss for biased samples from the minority class. After optimizing the combined loss for prediction and fairness, the important weights responsible for bias and misclassification are calculated locally between clients. These weight indices are shared using secure aggregation to calculate the final aggregation weightage. Further, a secure aggregation scheme is devised that penalises biased weights across clients, rather than punishing the global model. FairWeight is evaluated on five real-world datasets and compared against five state-of-the-art fairness-aware aggregation schemes. The results provide empirical evidence that FairWeight achieves both balanced accuracy and fairness, with reduced computation and communication overheads. | Harsh Kasyap; Ugur Ilker Atmaca; Carsten Maple; Nic Lane | federated learning, fairness, secure aggregation, model interpretation, bias mitigation | private fairness-aware aggregation | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TSC.2026.3686323) |

| 94/220 | ⭐ | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TSC | A | `AdpFL: A Privacy-Preserving Federated Learning Framework Through Adaptive Model Pruning on Non-IID Data` - Federated learning (FL) has shown great potential, especially with the rise of complex foundation models and growing privacy needs. AdpFL conducts an exploration phase prior to the formal FL process, during which each local dataset’s deviation and corresponding threshold are computed locally. These results are then utilised to perform model pruning during the formal FL training. By doing so, the method achieves full adaptivity while simultaneously addressing challenges related to communications costs and non-IID data. The extensive experiments on various datasets and FL tasks demonstrate that our method not only efficiently accelerates global convergence but also achieves superior accuracy and robustness compared to traditional methods. AdpFL reduces computational overhead and decreases the effective update size (i.e., the proportion of non-zero parameters) by approximately half while improving global accuracy by up to 20%, and it also lowers the accuracy of white-box MIA attacks across various models. | 95/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TIFS | A | `FedPSA: Adaptive Backdoor Attacks on Federated Large Language Models via Poisoning Sensitive Prompt Layers` - Federated Learning (FL) provides a privacy-preserving paradigm for collaborative training of Large Language Models (LLMs) across distributed institutions. | 96/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TIFS | A | `GHOST: GAN-Harnessed Optimization for Strategic Backdoor Persistence With Future Update Anticipation in Non-IID Federated Learning` - Federated learning (FL) enables collaborative training without sharing raw data, but its decentralized nature exposes it to backdoor attacks that embed hidden malicious behavior while preserving main-task accuracy. | 98/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `Risk-Aware Federated Alignment for Trustworthy and Controllable Language-Guided Diffusion Editing` - Language-guided diffusion models provide a flexible interface for controllable image editing, but the data required for model adaptation are often distributed across institutions and cannot be centrally pooled because of privacy and governance constraints. However, intermittent connectivity between low Earth orbit (LEO) satellites and ground stations (GSs), coupled with resource constraints, significantly hinders distributed training of large-scale models, such as split learning (SL). To address these challenges, we first model key factors to simulate complex STINs, including service differences, radio jamming, and data heterogeneity. Then we propose an online knowledge distillation (OKD)-driven split learning (SL) framework, termed OKD-SL, which not only ensures continuous training but also effectively mitigates class bias. To enable distributed strategies, a multi-objective problem is formulated as a decentralized partially observable Markov decision process (Dec-POMDP), jointly optimizing model offloading, resource allocation, and soft-label scheduling in an end-to-end manner. Furthermore, we introduce a stepwise unified reward shaping (SURS) method to strengthen interdependency between stages. To adaptively capture graph-structured topology, we propose a graph attention network (GAT)-enhanced multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) solution with a multi-head sharing network, achieving spatial awareness without decoupling operations. Extensive experiments validate the effectiveness of the proposed framework and solution, achieving a 9.9% improvement in test accuracy with enhanced robustness, a 26.2% reduction in training latency, a 42.7% reduction in class imbalance, and superior overall performance compared with vanilla baselines. | Rong Yuan; Shaohua Wu; Chengjia Lei; Qinyu Zhang | split learning, online knowledge distillation, satellite-terrestrial integrated networks, multi-agent reinforcement learning, resource allocation | split learning for satellite-terrestrial sensing classification | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3736849) |
| 100/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `CREEMFL: Cross-modal Retrieval Enhanced Energy-efficient Multimodal Federated Learning in Wireless Networks` - By integrating information across multiple modalities, multimodal federated learning (FL) often outperforms unimodal FL in areas such as healthcare and intelligent sensing. At the wireless edge, devices have limited energy and bandwidth resources and often lack certain modalities. This not only increases statistical heterogeneity but also slows convergence speed and increases the number of global rounds, leading to higher overall energy consumption. We present CREEMFL (Cross-modal Retrieval Enhanced Energy-efficient Multimodal Federated Learning), an energy-aware multimodal FL framework that uses cross-modal retrieval as data augmentation. In the proposed approach, the core idea is selective imputation: for a subset of samples with missing modalities, data is retrieved and supplemented from the public dataset, and the remaining samples are zero-padded to limit computation. We analytically characterize how the retrieval ratios affect a sufficient number of global rounds to reach a target training tolerance under the stated assumptions. This characterization motivates a system-level energy model to balance fewer rounds against lower per-round energy cost. CREEMFL then solves a two-level optimization. The outer layer selects retrieval ratios to minimize total energy, and the inner layer jointly allocates communication and computation resources under orthogonal uplink access. We provide closed-form or semi-closed-form expressions for transmission time, bandwidth, transmit power, and central processing unit (CPU) frequency that conditionally minimize energy under latency and wireless constraints. Experiments on the MIMIC-CXR chest X-ray radiology report dataset show that CREEMFL outperforms the baseline model by achieving higher accuracy, shorter completion time, and lower total energy consumption. Additional experiments on CMU-MOSEI show higher mean test accuracy across three client modality compositions and demonstrate the energy–deadline tradeoff for text, audio, and vision. | Jingyuan Liu; Zheng Chang; Yunlong Lu; Geyong Min; Yan Zhang | multimodal federated learning, cross-modal retrieval, missing modalities, energy efficiency, wireless networks | energy-efficient multimodal federated learning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3743015) |
| 101/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `LeoTune: Task Offloading and Adaptive Migration for Multi-Task Federated Fine-Tuning in LEO Satellite-Terrestrial Integrated Networks` - The large-scale deployment of Low Earth Orbit (LEO) satellites enables distributed AI tasks within satellite-terrestrial integrated networks (STIN), while federated learning (FL) has addressed the challenges of data privacy protection and communication latency of the distributed AI tasks in terrestrial networks. This work introduces a LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation)-based federated fine-tuning (FFT) tailored for STIN to reduce computational and communication overhead. Specifically, we formulate the joint task offloading and migration problem as a mixed- integer nonlinear programming (MINLP) model aimed at minimizing energy consumption and latency. | 102/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `FedATMV: Towards Heterogeneous Hybrid Federated Learning via Adaptive Server-Side Training and Model Variation` - With the explosive growth of distributed data sources and increasing concerns over data privacy, Federated Learning (FL) has emerged as a promising paradigm for privacy-preserving, distributed model training. | 103/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Accelerating Hierarchical Federated Learning under Mobility via Model Migration in Cloud-Edge-End Collaborative Networks` - Deploying hierarchical federated learning (HFL) in cloud-edge-end collaborative networks has become a mainstream approach for training models without exposing raw data. Experimental results demonstrate that HFL-M3 achieves an average reduction of 12.68% in rounds and 9.73% in latency compared to benchmark frameworks across various Non-IID settings on three datasets. | Jian Tang; Xiaoyu Xia; Ibrahim Khalil; Mengsha Kou; Minghui Liwang; Jer Shyuan Ng; Xiuhua Li; Xianbin Wang | hierarchical federated learning, client mobility, model migration, non-IID, cloud-edge-end collaboration | mobility-aware hierarchical federated learning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3748371) |
| 104/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `SAFL: SWIPT-Aided Federated Deep Reinforcement Learning with Layer-Wise Aggregation in 5G and Beyond` - Federated Deep Reinforcement Learning (FDRL) has been regarded as an emerging paradigm to empower various distributed Machine/Deep Learning (ML/DL) in 5G and Beyond. | 105/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Joint Offloading and Layer Splitting for Resource-Limited Split Federated Learning` - The growing scale of deep models is straining learning at the edge: resource-constrained devices struggle to train locally, and heterogeneous wireless links create communication bottlenecks, resulting in stragglers. The problem is NP-hard, and we apply an exact finite-interval decomposition with interval-level pruning, which yields a globally optimal solution when the fixed-interval subproblems are solved to optimality, or a certified anytime solution under a time limit. Extensive experiments on the MNIST, CIFAR-10, and CIFAR-100 datasets with ResNet-18, together with an architecture study on CIFAR-10 using VGG-11 and MobileNetV2, validate the effectiveness of the proposed MESFL framework. We compare MESFL with seven baseline methods in terms of test accuracy and training latency. We further conduct an ablation analysis to study the effects of offloading and layer splitting decisions. | Shu Hong; Vincent W.S. | 106/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Invariant Federated Learning for Edge Intelligence: Mitigating Heterogeneity and Asynchrony via Exit Strategy and Invariant Penalty` - This paper presents an invariant federated learning framework for resource-constrained edge intelligence. Accordingly, we propose a practical exit strategy based on client utility and delay, which removes such clients from the main training loop to protect in-distribution (ID) performance. | 107/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `FLR-SS: Accelerating Federated Large Language Model LoRA Tuning by Structural Sparsification` - Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable performance across a wide range of tasks; however, training them from scratch requires substantial computational resources. However, directly applying LoRA in FL can still incur substantial communication overhead, particularly in wireless environments. To address this, we propose a novel framework, called Federated LoRA with Structural Sparsification (FLR-SS), which reduces communication cost by sparsifying LoRA matrices through the removal of rows and columns with the smallest norms, thereby preserving the most informative components. Sheng; Ennan Zhai; Laizhong Cui | federated learning, large language models, LoRA, structural sparsification, communication efficiency | communication-efficient federated LoRA tuning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3752635) |
| 108/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `FedEL++: Efficient Federated Learning for Heterogeneous Clients` - Federated learning (FL) allows distributed devices to collaboratively train models while preserving data privacy, but heterogeneous computation and communication resources create stragglers, slowing training and limiting scalability. We propose FedEL++, a federated elastic learning framework that addresses both computation and communication heterogeneity. FedEL++ introduces a globally coordinated sliding window, anchored to the slowest client, and optimizes block window selection across all clients under a unified runtime constraint. | 109/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `FedTADA: Trajectory-Aware Displacement Aggregation for Sparse Federated Learning` - Federated Learning in Internet-of-Things networks confronts a fundamental spatio-temporal trade-off driven by statistical heterogeneity and severe client participation sparsity. Traditional optimization frameworks struggle between two suboptimal extremes: instantaneous aggregation in Drop mode, which suffers from persistent sampling variance due to tiny active client subsets, and historical caching in Stale mode, which incurs cumulative spatial divergence and staleness bias from outdated local updates. In this paper, we propose Federated Trajectory-Aware Displacement Aggregation (FedTADA), an optimization framework formulated in cumulative displacement space. To stabilize scale normalization without stochastic feedback loops, FedTADA introduces a zero-order probing phase that establishes an exogenous energy reference anchor. Guided by an unnormalized negative entropy regularizer, we derive a scale-equivariant exponential controller that dynamically balances instantaneous descent against historical consensus. Theoretical analysis proves that FedTADA achieves higher-order variance suppression, accelerating the client-sampling variance decay rate from the standard $\mathcal {O}(1/T)$ down to $\mathcal {O}(1/T^{2})$ via a joint Lyapunov potential function that strictly neutralizes staleness bias while isolating the irreducible Local-SGD drift floor. Extensive experiments across four distinct modalities under fully trainable end-to-end convolutional architectures demonstrate that FedTADA achieves significant accuracy improvements over state-of-the-art baselines under extreme label skew with zero additional client-side computational or communication overhead. | Xianyao You; Tingting Wu; Hao Zhang; Wei Zhang; Siyao Cheng; Jie Liu | federated learning, partial participation, displacement aggregation, staleness, convergence analysis, Internet of Things | trajectory-aware aggregation under sparse participation | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3752495) |
| 110/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Mitigating Instability and Knowledge Omission in Over-the-Air Federated Continual Learning` - Over-the-air federated learning (OTA-FL) enables simultaneous aggregation of local model updates but is typically designed for stationary learning tasks. Extending OTA-FL to federated continual learning introduces two coupled challenges: task transitions can abruptly amplify local updates and aggravate analog aggregation distortion, while newly emerging knowledge may be localized at weak-channel clients and repeatedly omitted by communication-oriented scheduling. We propose a stable and knowledge-aware OTA-FCL framework. A client-specific scale-constrained stabilizer controls abnormal update amplification while preserving update direction, and a low-bit cross-entropy compatibility state is locally constructed to identify insufficiently assimilated local knowledge. The server applies lexicographic compatibility-and-age scheduling and jointly optimizes client selection and OTA transceiver variables only among clients with identical learning priority. We further derive a nonconvex convergence bound that characterizes learning, selection, stabilization, OTA aggregation, and task-variation errors. | 111/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Joint Route Scheduling and Adaptive Data Offloading for Federated Edge Learning in STIN` - In Satellite-Terrestrial Integrated Networks (STIN), distributed Federated Edge Learning (FEL) has become mainstream methods for data processing and analysis. The traditional FEL algorithm assigns devices for local training and satellites for aggregation to perform intelligent prediction and decision-making. However, it adopts a synchronous aggregation mechanism, where model updating is performed only after all devices complete training. Considering the heterogeneous computational frequency of devices and the advanced onboard processing capabilities of satellites, satellites act as participants in collaborative local training. In this paper, we introduce a Data Offloading-enabled Federated Edge Learning (DO-FEL) framework, where devices offload part of their data to Low Earth Orbit (LEO) satellites. The LEO satellites also act as Edge Servers (ESs), while the Geostationary Earth Orbit (GEO) satellite performs global aggregation. In DO-FEL, the dynamic satellite-device visibility and the limited computational frequency of a single satellite incur additional delay for FEL in STIN. Based on this, we propose a Dynamic Route Scheduling (DRS) strategy to optimize multisatellites' assistance for the collaborative training. Meanwhile, we propose an Adaptive Joint Delay-Energy Optimization (AJDEO) strategy. For different task requirements, the AJDEO strategy adjusts the devices' offloading proportion to balance overall delay and energy consumption. Experimental results show that with the DRS strategy, the DO-FEL framework reduces satelliteside delay and further reduces the overall delay. Besides, the proposed framework with two strategies achieves the trade-off between the overall delay and energy consumption compared with other baselines. This paper provides a new perspective for efficient FEL in STIN through flexible data offloading under actual satellite-device visibility. | Yatong Zhang; Weixiao Meng; Shuyi Chen | federated edge learning, satellite-terrestrial integrated networks, data offloading, route scheduling, LEO satellites | data offloading for federated edge learning in STIN | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3753237) |
| 112/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Pull-in Aggregation Protocol: A Receiver-Oriented Decentralized Federated Learning Paradigm` - Decentralized Federated Learning (DFL) trains models collaboratively without a central server, but asymmetric and time-varying communication often leads to imbalanced information flow and biased aggregation. Most existing methods address this issue with push-sum normalization, which requires maintaining auxiliary normalization states and can accumulate numerical drift, thereby slowing mixing and degrading convergence under limited connectivity. To address this, we propose Pull-in protocol, a receiver-driven decentralized learning frame work for directed time-varying graphs. Instead of relying on indiscriminate broadcasting, each receiver actively pulls models from a budgeted subset of incoming neighbors and constructs a row-stochastic aggregation vector locally. This receiver-side normalization makes the model aggregation step a stable convex combination by design and avoids push-sum-type normaliza tion variables in directed model mixing. To mitigate the bias caused by directed and asymmetric aggregation, we incorporate a gradient-tracking correction mechanism. In Pull-in-GT, the model aggregation matrix is row-stochastic, while the tracking matrix is constructed to be column-stochastic, enabling average preservation of the tracking variable over directed graphs. We establish convergence guarantees under standard smooth non convex objectives and bounded stochastic noise. The resulting bound explicitly separates optimization progress from consensus induced error terms and yields the rate O(T<sup>−1/2</sup>) quantifies the mixing speed of the induced row-stochastic matrix. Extensive experiments on vision-centric decentralized learning benchmarks demonstrate that Pull-in consistently improves training stability and achieves stronger performance than representative push-sum based DFL baselines across a wide range of network asymmetries. | Fang Shi; JianKang Zeng; Shunpu Tang; Weiwei Lin; Qiong Huang; Li Shen | decentralized federated learning, directed graphs, receiver-driven aggregation, row-stochastic mixing, gradient tracking | receiver-oriented decentralized federated learning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3752625) |
| 113/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Semi-Asynchronous Federated Learning with Heterogeneity-Aware Coordination Mechanism in Mobile Edge Computing Networks` - Federated learning (FL) enables edge devices (EDs) in mobile edge computing (MEC) networks to collaboratively train a shared model without exchanging raw data. However, heterogeneous computing and communication capabilities, non-independent and identically distributed (non-IID) data, and limited wireless resources introduce straggler delays, stale updates, and client drift, thereby increasing convergence latency. To address these challenges, we propose SAFL-HACM, a semi-asynchronous FL framework with a heterogeneity-aware coordination mechanism for MEC networks. SAFL-HACM allows EDs to train independently and buffers their completed updates for selective aggregation without interrupting ongoing local training. It coordinates three components: 1) a local drift control mechanism that combines update-direction correction with a stateful drift variable to mitigate non-IID-induced drift and stale-model inconsistency; 2) a cluster-balanced scheduling strategy that jointly considers data-distribution representation, update staleness, local training utility, and participation history; and 3) a latency-aware bandwidth allocation scheme that minimizes the completion time of each aggregation cycle under a limited uplink bandwidth budget. Experiments across four benchmark datasets and different levels of data heterogeneity show that SAFL-HACM reduces convergence latency by 64.0% on average over representative baselines. Moreover, across different device scales, it improves test accuracy by an average of 6.5% while maintaining robust performance. | Hushuang Zeng; Xiuhua Li; Guozeng Xu; Jinlong Hao; Xiaofei Wang; Victor C. M. Leung | semi-asynchronous federated learning, mobile edge computing, client drift, staleness, device scheduling | heterogeneity-aware semi-asynchronous federated learning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3753963) |
| 114/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `FASAFL: Fairness Aware Starlink Assisted Federated Learning for Communication Heterogeneous Edge Networks` - Federated learning over heterogeneous edge net works is often limited by persistent communication imbalance. Devices with weak terrestrial connectivity may repeatedly miss upload deadlines and become underrepresented in global training. Existing terrestrial scheduling methods can improve resource utilization, but they still operate within the available ground network and cannot always recover devices whose terrestrial links remain infeasible. Satellite assisted federated learning provides wider communication coverage, but existing studies mainly focus on satellite resource coordination, hierarchical aggregation, or data offloading. They provide limited support for selectively using satellite access to recover long term participation of communication constrained ground devices. To address this problem, we propose FASAFL, a fairness aware Starlink assisted federated learning framework. FASAFL explicitly models Starlink as a dynamic and resource constrained uplink by incorporating satellite visibility, shared beam capacity, handover, gateway routing, weather conditions, and retransmission reliability into the access decision. The server selectively assigns Starlink to devices whose terrestrial links restrict timely participation, rather than treating satellite access as a uniformly better communication option. FASAFL further integrates learning value, resource expenditure, and participation history into the scheduling process, so that current satellite allocation is jointly coordinated with the long term participation of communication constrained devices. The server also uses bounded quality aware compensation during scheduling and aggregation to increase the opportunities of persistently underrepresented devices without giving excessive influence to stale or low quality updates. Experiments on four datasets with Hypatia and ns-3 based dynamic LEO simulations show that FASAFL improves effective participation, learning performance, fairness, and deadline reliability under limited satellite resources. | Yuncan Tang; Lina Ni; Jinquan Zhang; Yongquan Liang | federated learning, satellite-assisted networks, Starlink, fairness, participation scheduling | fairness-aware satellite-assisted federated learning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3754357) |
| 115/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `SemFedVAE: A Communication-Efficient Federated Learning Framework for Variational Autoencoder With Semantic Latent Space Alignment` - Federated training of variational autoencoders (VAEs) must share distributional knowledge across clients without centralizing data or repeatedly exchanging high-dimensional model parameters. Under non-IID data, full-parameter federated optimization is communication-heavy, and clients can learn incompatible latent spaces that slow convergence and degrade reconstruction and generation. We propose SemFedVAE, a communication-efficient, personalized federated VAE framework in which clients keep their own encoders and decoders and share only latent posterior statistics. The server aggregates the semantic means and standard deviations, combining scales in the log-variance domain, into a global latent target. A global consistency (GC) term in the evidence lower bound (ELBO) aligns each local latent distribution with this target by minimizing their KL divergence. We analyze the communication complexity and show that, for bounded GC weights, the regularizer preserves the standard non-convex convergence order. Experiments on six datasets with 5–20 clients show that SemFedVAE reduces the per-round training payload by four to five orders of magnitude relative to full-parameter protocols that train a shareable global model, while remaining competitive in reconstruction and generation quality; a client without local data instead requires a one-time model transfer. Ablations compare the aggregation rule with variance-based alternatives and examine the GC strength and the label-aware extension. | Jincheng Cai; Qinglin Zhao; Jian Li; Li Feng; Xu Chen; Jian Zhu; Ni Zhao | federated learning, variational autoencoder, latent space alignment, communication efficiency, personalization | communication-efficient federated VAE training | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3755564) |
| 116/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `AFIND+: Adaptive Client-Centric Collaboration for Decentralized Personalized Federated Learning` - Decentralized personalized federated learning (DPFL) enables devices to exchange knowledge over peer-to-peer links while retaining client-specific models. Experiments on heterogeneous image and text benchmarks show improved accuracy and fewer rounds to a target accuracy relative to the evaluated DPFL baselines. | 117/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Adaptive Quantization and Device Scheduling for Asynchronous Federated Learning Over Resource-Constrained Wireless Networks` - Federated learning (FL) is a promising distributed learning paradigm that enables multiple devices to collaboratively train a global model without sharing local private data. Considering the device heterogeneity in computation and communication capabilities, asynchronous aggregation is introduced in FL to improve the learning efficiency. However, asynchronous FL suffers from communication bottlenecks due to limited wireless resources. To address this issue, we propose AQAFL, an adaptive quantization method for asynchronous FL, while also integrating device scheduling and wireless resource allocation. We first present a convergence analysis of asynchronous FL with quantized transmission, and then formulate an optimization problem to minimize the convergence upper bound under long-term latency and bandwidth constraints by jointly optimizing the quantization bit width, device scheduling, and wireless bandwidth allocation. Following that, we analytically characterize the optimal bandwidth allocation and prove that it can be obtained via bisection search, and derive the closed-form expression for the quantization bit width. Subsequently, an efficient device scheduling strategy is proposed to determine the number of participating devices in each round. Experiments show that compared with other methods, AQAFL reduces the total communication overhead by up to 69% and the overhead required to achieve the same target test accuracy by 32.6%–88.6%, while improving test accuracy by 4.1%–6.5%. | Xiaodong Li; Yulong Gao; Fengyu Liang; Carla Fabiana Chiasserini | asynchronous federated learning, adaptive quantization, device scheduling, bandwidth allocation, convergence analysis | adaptive quantization for asynchronous wireless federated learning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3755226) |
| 118/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Asynchronous Collaboration in Personalized Federated Learning under Device Heterogeneity` - Collaboration-based personalized federated learning (Co-PFL) achieves superior accuracy on non-IID data by leveraging a collaboration graph that captures pairwise similarities among clients for personalized model aggregation. A natural alternative is to process client updates asynchronously. Yet, asynchrony introduces staleness that distorts both collaboration graph estimation and personalized model aggregation, two coupled components in Co-PFL. Existing asynchronous solutions only handle staleness for a single global model and fail to preserve this coupling. To bridge this gap, we propose PACE, an asynchronous Co- PFL framework that jointly manages staleness in collaboration estimation and model aggregation. PACE introduces three serverside designs: depth-calibrated collaboration estimation for heterogeneous model depths, buffer update with hierarchical decay for both collaboration relevance and staleness, and stalenesstriggered model multicast to proactively refresh selected clients when collaboration error becomes large. Together, these designs also enable PACE to support model heterogeneity, where clients may train submodels of different depths. | 119/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `Budget-Constrained Federated Bandits for Mobile Applications` - With the development of federated learning and mobile computing, federated bandit frameworks have been proposed to enable multiple clients to explore and exploit collaboratively under the guarantee of data privacy. For FBwK, we propose a new definition of OPT solution and a bandit algorithm, namely FedUCBwK. | 121/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `Decentralizing Compressed Sensing for Federated Learning with Hardware–Software Codesign` - Compressed Sensing (CS), a sparse signal compression technique, has been adopted in federated learning (FL) reduce to network communication overhead. | 122/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `Deploying Over-the-Air Federated Learning in Real-World Multi-Antenna Systems` - Over-the-air federated learning (OTA-FL) harnesses the superposition property of wireless channels for efficient model aggregation, offering significant spectrum savings. | 123/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `Entropy-Adaptive Federated Learning with Efficient Bit Allocation over Wireless Channels` - A key bottleneck in federated learning (FL) is the high communication cost of transmitting large local model updates. | 125/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `FLUDE: An Efficient Federated Learning Framework with Undependable Devices` - In a federated learning (FL) system, many devices, such as smartphones, are often undependable (e.g., frequently disconnected from WiFi) during training. | 129/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `FedKRSO: Communication and Memory Efficient Federated Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models` - Fine-tuning is essential to adapt general-purpose large language models (LLMs) to domain-specific tasks. | 131/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `Less is More: Persistent Low-Frequency Backdoor Injection in Federated Learning` - Federated learning (FL) enables multiple clients to collaboratively train a machine learning model without sharing their local data. In this paper, we investigate why backdoor attacks become less effective under low-frequency injection and propose a novel attack paradigm for FL, called REinforced Memorization-based INterval backDoor attack (REMIND). | 133/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `Mixing Matrix Design for Energy-efficient Decentralized Federated Learning` - We consider the design of mixing matrices to minimize the operation cost for decentralized federated learning (DFL) in wireless networks, with focus on minimizing the maximum per-node energy consumption. As a critical hyperparameter for DFL, the mixing matrix controls both the convergence rate and the needs of agent-to-agent communications, and has thus been studied extensively. However, existing designs mostly focused on minimizing the communication time, leaving open the per-node energy consumption that is critical for energy-constrained devices. This work addresses this gap through a theoretically-justified solution for mixing matrix design that aims at minimizing the maximum per-node energy consumption until convergence, while taking into account the broadcast nature of wireless communications. | 134/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `Optimizing Split Federated Learning through Adaptive Pipeline Parallelism` - Split federated learning (SFL) offers a promising solution for training large models on resource-constrained edge devices. However, existing synchronous and asynchronous SFL frameworks are plagued by a fundamental sequential execution pattern for communication and computation. This design creates a severe communication bottleneck, particularly for modern large models with substantial activation sizes, leading to significant resource underutilization and extended training time. To dismantle this bottleneck, we propose MicroSFL, a novel SFL framework with adaptive pipeline parallelism. MicroSFL enables workers to divide each of their training mini-batches into multiple small micro-batches and process them in a pipelined fashion. To counteract the challenges of system and statistical heterogeneity, MicroSFL adaptively assigns distinct micro-batch sizes to workers to accommodate their heterogeneous capacities, maximizing the temporal overlap between the computation and transmission of activations/gradients. Besides, to address statistical heterogeneity, MicroSFL employs an adaptive updating strategy. This strategy accumulates gradients at the server side to simulate updates on a large, balanced batch. Guided by a theoretical convergence analysis, the optimal updating weights are assigned to different workers to normalize their contributions for model updating. MicroSFL then dynamically determines the appropriate timing to apply the aggregated gradients, ensuring both stable convergence and high model accuracy. | 135/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `PFAE: Personalized Federated Learning for Anomaly Detection Over Heterogeneous IoT Domains` - Existing federated learning (FL)-based anomaly detection (AD) methods suffer from performance degradation due to domain heterogeneity, e.g., differences in features, data distributions, and data types across clients from different domains. To address this problem, we propose Personalized Federated Heterogeneous Autoencoder (PFAE), an AD framework that introduces a private head for processing domain-specific inputs and a public backbone for shared representation learning. We theoretically prove that the difference in the expected anomaly score of PFAE calculated using the public backbone for benign samples from any pair of clients is bounded. This implies that the distribution of benign samples is shared across domains, so benign samples from a client may not be misidentified as anomalies by other clients during inference. PFAE has lower training complexity than existing autoencoder-based methods. | 136/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `The Aggregated Model is a Confounder: Enabling Deconfounded Federated Learning for OOD Generalization` - Federated Learning (FL) enables collaborative model training across edge devices without sharing raw data. However, most FL approaches suffer from severe performance degradation when deployed to unseen edges with distribution shifts, known as the out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization problem. Given that causal relationships between inputs and labels are typically invariant under OOD scenarios, causality-inspired FL has attracted increasing attention. Existing works primarily focus on eliminating spurious correlations within each client’s local data while overlooking a critical step in FL, model aggregation. We show that in a structural causal graph, the aggregated model is a confounder leading to reliance on spurious correlations. To address this problem, we introduce a Federated Deconfounding learning (FedDEC) framework based on backdoor adjustment to eliminate the confounding paths. Theoretically, backdoor adjustment requires enumerating aggregated models under all possible aggregation weights, which is infeasible in practice. To efficiently approximate this process, FedDEC proposes a client-side disentangled feature learning module that separates personalized and global components. It also introduces a deconfounded training module that computes cross-attention between the personalized features and a server-maintained prototype bank to obtain deconfounded causal features. Experiments demonstrate that FedDEC achieves higher accuracy while maintaining comparable convergence speed compared to state-of-the-art algorithms. | Jiayuan Zhang; Xuefeng Liu; Jianwei Niu; Haotian Yang; Wanyu Lin; Xinghao Wu | federated learning, out-of-distribution generalization, causal inference, deconfounding, model aggregation | deconfounded federated learning for OOD generalization | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/INFOCOM59046.2026.11571547) |
| 137/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `Towards Asynchronous Client Collaboration in Personalized Federated Learning` - Collaboration-based personalized federated learning (Co-PFL) achieves superior accuracy on non-IID data by leveraging a collaboration graph that captures pairwise similarities among client data distributions for personalized model aggregation. However, its synchronous operation suffers from substantial delays caused by slow clients, limiting its applicability in latency-sensitive IoT applications. A natural alternative is asynchronous Co-PFL, where the server processes model updates as they arrive. | 138/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | INFOCOM | A | `ZorBA: Zeroth-order Federated Fine-tuning of LLMs with Heterogeneous Block Activation` - Federated fine-tuning of large language models (LLMs) enables collaborative tuning across distributed clients. | 140/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TIFS | A | `Spa: Stealthy and Persistent Backdoor Attacks in Federated Learning via Feature-Space Alignment` - Federated Learning (FL) has emerged as a leading paradigm for privacy-preserving machine learning, yet the distributed nature of FL introduces unique security challenges, notably the threat of backdoor attacks. | 146/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TDSC | A | `Data Re-valuation in Vertical Federated Learning with Importance Sampling: A Case Study in Blockchain` - Vertical federated learning (VFL) enables multiple parties with vertically partitioned data to collaboratively train machine learning models while preserving privacy. | 156/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Toward Personalized Federated Learning via Overlapping Coalition Formation Game` - To tackle the challenge of data heterogeneity in federated learning (FL), personalized FL has been proposed to maximize individual utility (model performance) by customizing personalized models for clients. However, they assume that clients can participate in only one collaborative coalition, constraining players’ attempts to join multiple coalitions. Different from prior works, we approach personalized FL from the perspective of hedonic overlapping coalition formation (OCF) games where rational clients can join multiple coalitions and generate their personalized model by weighting the local and coalition models. Specifically, a number of personalized learning objectives of different clients in PFL are considered as multiple tasks. | 158/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Boosting Gradient-Based Training Diagnosis for Efficient and Accurate Federated Learning` - Federated Learning (FL) allows edge clients to collaborate in model training with data privacy preserved, yet it is known to suffer low training efficiency and model accuracy. Given that efficiency and accuracy are usually conflicting objectives, existing practices increasingly employ an adaptive scheme that changes the FL configurations (e.g., quantization or sparsification level) based on runtime training status, for which accurate training diagnosis—used for guiding the optimization actions—is crucial. However, while training diagnosis is a common task shared by different optimization schemes, existing works propose their diagnosis methods in an ad-hoc manner, which yield multiple limitations. First, the diagnosis metric in an optimization scheme may sometimes be less accurate than others; second, existing schemes fail to fully exploit the diagnosis result by applying it for only one optimization action; third, existing methods usually do not perceive cross-client data heterogeneity, failing to simultaneously enhance FL accuracy. To tackle those limitations, we make a systematical study on the training diagnosis methods of multiple optimization schemes, and propose metric grafting—replacing a scheme’s diagnosis metric with a better one to improve the training performance. Moreover, to fully exploit the potential of training diagnosis, we build a system platform that supports flexible combinations of training diagnosis and optimization actions (i.e., single-diagnosis-multiple-actions and multiple-diagnosis-multiple-actions). Evaluation on testbeds show that, with metric grafting and advanced diagnosis-action combinations, we can substantially improve the efficiency and accuracy performance of FL. | Jiayi Zhang; Zuo Gan; Chen Chen; Zhifeng Jiang; Hao Wang; Yifei Zhu; Quan Chen; Minyi Guo | federated learning, training diagnosis, adaptive optimization, quantization, sparsification | gradient-based training diagnosis for efficient federated learning | [➡️](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMC.2026.3673684) |
| 159/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Breaking the Bottleneck of Heterogeneity in Federated Learning Through GBG: Grouping, Block Training, and Global Distillation` - Federated learning (FL) collaboratively trains a global model across multiple clients without sharing local data, effectively utilizing data while preserving privacy. | 165/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `Communication-Efficient Multimodal Federated Learning: Joint Modality and Client Selection` - Multimodal federated learning (MFL) aims to enrich model training in FL settings where clients are collecting measurements across multiple modalities. | 167/220 |  | 2026 | 21/11/2026 | TMC | A | `AoI Minimization in Heterogeneous MEC Networks: A Federated Learning-Assisted Hybrid DRL and Convex Approach` - This paper investigates a dynamic heterogeneous mobile edge computing network (HMECN), where mobile devices (MDs) could offload their full tasks to a small base station (SBS) directly or the macro base station (MBS) in direct or relay mode.
